#### The run's parameters as a file — the TOML artefact direct mode reads
- **Status:** **fixes-applied, F1 committed — stop-and-ask before F2.** **All three of spec §7's
  sources now write the file the run used**, and the one the format exists for was the one that did
  not: a run scoring from a supplied file has `Supplied` calibrations and no rate map, and `of_run`
  *panicked* on it (measured before anything was built on it —
  `read group 0's calibration is Supplied and no rate was offered for it`).
  `ReadsBehindEachCalibration` has three constructors, one a source. `to_run_parameters_for` takes
  `Option<&CensusIdentity>` — **direct mode has no census** — and `None` keeps the file's warrants,
  which spec §2.1 settles rather than taste: it rejected demoting on every read *because it breaks
  the two-mode oracle*, and direct mode is exactly the mode with no census. The answer is a
  three-state `CensusAgreement`, because *they agree* and *nothing was compared* are not one state
  and F2 reads it. A **demoted** run writes back the terms it read, and the file it writes is
  stable. The file is written by `write_beside_the_vcf`, at `<the VCF's stem>.parameters.toml`,
  atomically.
- **⚑ ONE THING IS YOURS, and it is why this stops before F2.** F1 added a line at the top of every
  file saying how many of its seven groups of numbers were fitted. **All three reviews found the
  same defect**: it said *"fitted from your data"*, and a file demoted under §2.1 — every warrant
  `supplied` — still counted five groups as fitted. One predicate was simply wrong and is fixed
  (`SubstitutionRateRow.rate` **does** carry a warrant, and the demotion moves it). **The other four
  groups carry no *handed-over* state at all** — a slippage row carries a smoothing origin, a length
  spectrum a rung, a contamination row which reads it was fitted from, the prior's seed a rung — so
  `demoted_to_no_better_than_supplied` cannot reach them. **This is the same gap D3 already recorded
  for `SeedRung::FittedCurve`**, which after a demotion still reads *"both moments came off the run's
  own fitted population curve"*. F1 **discloses** it in the file's own prose rather than overclaiming,
  and `a_demoted_file_stops_counting_the_groups_that_carry_a_warrant` pins exactly which three move
  and which four stay. Closing it is a change to what the file records: either a demoted file records
  that it was demoted, or those four gain a warrant. **F2 makes `RunParameterReport` a view over the
  file, so it inherits whichever you choose.**
- **Three more from the reviews, none blocking.** (1) **The design review recommends one sentence in
  spec §2.1 or §6** saying that a run with no census keeps the file's warrants — the code argues it
  from §2.1's rationale, and a decision taken inside a step should not live only in a doc comment.
  (2) It also observes that `run_streaming.md` §2 gives psp mode's **calling** stage the psps only,
  so a separately-invoked psp-mode call has no `CensusIdentity` either — **on today's spec the fourth
  binding may be reachable from no real run at all**, while §13 test 5 asks for the demotion to be
  visible in a run's report. (3) The reader review argues the repeat-tract fallback should be
  disclosed on a *partly* covered table and not only a wholly empty one — the golden file writes 1
  substitution rate for 9 `(read group × stratum)` cells and its section never mentions the stated
  0.001. **E3 decided the opposite deliberately and recorded why**; not overturned.
- **⚑ The command surface's, not this file's — asked and answered.** The file records no run date, no
  caller version and no command line, so two copies in a directory are told apart only by their
  names. What a run stamps into its own output is a property of the invocation, and spec §11 already
  puts the neighbouring question (`dump-parameters`) with the rest of `pop_var_caller_exp`'s
  subcommands.
- **Two corrections to the record.** A design-review number did not survive checking: it refuted the
  header's *"eleven lines of prose"* correctly (it is **39**, already fixed in the source before the
  review returned) but its replacement — *106 identical lines, first difference at 107* — was measured
  against `ng_parameters_file_e2_defaults_run_as_written_2026-08-31.toml`, an artefact written at
  **E2** whose preamble E3 later changed; that comparison first differs at line 23. Measured on the
  base commit's own golden, the first `warrant` value is on **line 105**. And one correctness Minor
  was **declined with a reason**: re-adding the fitted-with-no-count guard to all three sources would
  panic on a legal file — a `fitted_here` multiplier with no `observations`, which `validate` accepts
  and which the new path round-trips and the old one did not.
- **Was: fixes-applied, Milestone E complete — at Checkpoint E, awaiting review.** **A run that fitted nothing and was handed no file assembles the parameters it scores with** (`RunParameters::of_defaults`), writes them to a file, reads that file back as the same run, and **says which of its numbers it guessed** — in its report and in the file's own prose. Milestone F is next: **F1 is where all three of spec §7's sources meet, and only two of them work today.** **Was: E1 and E2 committed.** **Every number a run takes from the binary is now a named constant with its origin beside it, and the whole set is written down in one place** (`parameters_file/defaults.rs`) — seven rows rather than spec §8's four, because `to_toml.rs`'s own `origins` module already carried the substitution rate, the slippage numbers and the inbreeding coefficient, and the last of those had no default anywhere until the owner's ruling of 2026-08-31 gave it one. **⚑ E1 also added a `validate` rung and removed it again**: a `defaulted` base-quality multiplier is not the compiled-in 1.0 the way the outlier weight is 0.01, because the multiplier's warrant is copied from the *error rate* it was built from — so the rung refused a file this caller had just written. **Both questions that raised are now ruled** (below): a library nothing could be fitted for keeps being charged the pre-pass's stated 0.001 rather than taken at its reported quality, and **spec §5's third row is the sentence to correct**; and a defaults run's inbreeding coefficient is **0**, settable for the whole run or per sample. E2 is next and is unblocked. **Was: fixes-applied, Milestone D complete — at Checkpoint D, awaiting review.** The two derived bindings of spec §3.1 are now produced from the run's own inputs rather than handed over as text: `CensusIdentity::of` mints one digested term per value the census refuses to pool across, in `first_disagreement`'s own words and order, and `of_run` takes the run's `ReferenceDigest` and spells it. **A file fitted from other inputs is refused naming a key of the file and both values; one fitted from another census of this cohort is used, and every number in it says it was handed over.** Milestone E is next, and **three questions below are the owner's before it starts** — the largest being that this milestone's own door cannot be called by direct mode, which has no census. **Was: fixes-applied, Milestone C complete — at Checkpoint C, awaiting review.** C1 through C5 are committed, with the key revision's last two names, the outlier weight's wiring and the two re-worded parser messages. **A file a run wrote reads back into the parameters that wrote it**, every float comes back the same double, and the five states of spec §5 each change an answer when collapsed. **Milestone D is next and two of its questions are open below.** Merged from `main` on 2026-08-30 (`077d38f3`), bringing the psp store and the quality numbers: 5,474 lib tests, 0 failed. **Was: fixes-applied, in Milestone C** — Milestones A and B complete, and C1 implemented, reviewed and fixes applied. **A file a run wrote reads back into the shape that wrote it, and a malformed one fails naming its line.** **Six questions are open and are the owner's** (below). Branch `ng-parameters-file`, from `main` at `a6e8472b`. **On direct mode's critical path**: [run_streaming.md](doc/devel/ng/spec/run_streaming.md) §2 cannot run without it.
- **Plan:** [parameters_file.md](doc/devel/ng/impl_plan/parameters_file.md) (A–F, 15 steps, 6 checkpoints); **Spec:** [parameters_file.md](doc/devel/ng/spec/parameters_file.md). **No architecture document, by the owner's decision of 2026-08-28** — the TOML key names are the coder's proposal and are revised against a file a fitted run produced, not on paper.
- **Code:** [src/ng/calling/parameters_file/](src/ng/calling/parameters_file/) — the file's shape, 26 structs and 8 enums, plus `testdata/every_shape.toml`, the golden copy every key is pinned against; `from_run_parameters.rs`, the projection from a run's assembled parameters, `to_toml.rs`, the hand-written writer with its own golden file `testdata/every_shape_as_written.toml`, and `from_toml.rs`, the reader and the line a failure sits on. `bindings.rs` derives the two bindings that are not names — `CensusIdentity::of` over a census's `RecordingTerms`, and the one hex spelling either binding's text is produced by. `ParametersFileError` lives in `mod.rs` rather than in the reader, because C2's `validate` and D2's bindings add variants from two other files. Reuse targets, unchanged: `RunParameters`, `Estimate<T>`, `Provenance`, `StratumFits`, the `toml` crate. B1 added seven read-only accessors to `run_parameters.rs` and three iterators to `stratum_fits.rs`, all of them so a writer can walk what a lookup can only answer one question about.
- **Impl reports:** [E3](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_e3_2026-08-31.md), [E2](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_e2_2026-08-31.md), [E1](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_e1_2026-08-31.md), [D3](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_d3_2026-08-31.md), [the bottom rung's warrant](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_bottom_rung_warrant_2026-08-30.md), [D2](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_d2_2026-08-30.md), [D1](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_d1_2026-08-30.md), [C2a](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_c2a_2026-08-30.md), [C1](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_c1_2026-08-30.md), [B3](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_b3_2026-08-28.md), [B2](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_b2_2026-08-28.md), [B1](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_b1_2026-08-28.md), [A1](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_a1_2026-08-28.md), [A2](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_a2_2026-08-28.md), [A3](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_parameters_file_a3_2026-08-28.md).
- **Latest reviews:** [E3](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e3_correctness_2026-08-31.md) — 0 Blockers / 9 Majors / ~10 Minors from three agents in isolated worktrees ([design fidelity](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e3_design_fidelity_2026-08-31.md), [a defaults run's file read as a PCR tomato geneticist](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e3_reader_2026-08-31.md)); **42 mutations** run across author and reviews, and the three that survived were all the same shape — a field or a guard whose only fixtures happened to agree. **The field's own headline predicate was one of them**: `every_tract_falls_back()` hard-coded to `true` passed all 5,563 library tests, because three tests asserted it true and none asserted it false. **The reader's Blocker was that "every repeat tract" reads as scope**, not as *one pair of numbers stands in for every stratum* — the fact that decides whether a mononucleotide call is dropped. [E2](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e2_correctness_2026-08-31.md) — 1 Blocker / 12 Majors / ~12 Minors from three agents in isolated worktrees ([design fidelity](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e2_design_fidelity_2026-08-31.md), [the defaults run's file read as a geneticist](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e2_reader_2026-08-31.md), whose [produced file](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e2_defaults_run_as_written_2026-08-31.toml) is kept beside it because nothing else in the tree shows one); 34 mutations run, 31 killed, 1 real weakening now pinned and 2 provably equivalent. **The Blocker was the file telling its reader the caller is broken** — every inbreeding row carried B3's pre-ruling text *"a run should not be able to write this line"*, and the geneticist's first action was to file a bug rather than change the number. **Three Majors were wrong claims of the author's, all caught by running the thing.** [E1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e1_correctness_2026-08-31.md) — 1 Blocker / 4 Majors / ~20 Minors from three agents in isolated worktrees ([design fidelity](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e1_design_fidelity_2026-08-31.md), [the file read as a geneticist](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_e1_reader_2026-08-31.md)); 15 mutations run, 13 killed, and the 2 survivors are pinned now. **The Blocker was this caller refusing a file it had just written** — the second time on this plan — and every other finding was prose that said something untrue, among them "a multiplier of one asserts nothing about the chemistry", which `read_likelihoods.md` §3.2 and `validate.rs` eleven lines above the rung both refute. [C5, the five states](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c5_2026-08-30.md) — 0 Blockers / 3 Majors / 6 Minors applied from one agent; five mutations run, five killed. **The Majors were two rows asserting through something the caller never reaches** — a fit-side function, and a warrant the writer copies with no logic between. [C4, the north-star round trip](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c4_2026-08-30.md) — 1 Blocker / 5 Majors / 10 Minors applied from one agent, which re-derived all three of the step's measured numbers and found all three right. **The Blocker was the fixture not being shaped like a fit's output**, which is the whole of what the owner's ruling rests on. [C3, the float oracle](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c3_2026-08-30.md) — 0 Blockers / 2 Majors / 5 Minors applied from one agent. **It found no defect in the code and two false claims in its own header**; the step's own value is the guard, since the writer that produces the artefact already formatted for round-trip by construction. [C2c, the two re-worded messages](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c2c_2026-08-30.md) — 1 Blocker / 7 Majors / 6 Minors applied from two agents. **The Blocker was the file writing its own error message**: the translation searched the parser's *rendering*, which echoes the offending line of the document, so a file with `invalid type: string` in a comment took its clause from its own text. [C2b](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c2b_2026-08-30.md) — 1 Blocker / 7 Majors / 11 Minors applied from three agents (correctness, design fidelity, and the file's own reader); 4 items recorded as owed. **The Blocker was the writer refusing itself**: a run with no repeat tracts declares no slippage group, and `validate` required that table to cover every read group — so Milestone E's defaults run and the single-sample case both wrote a file this caller turned down. [the outlier weight, wired](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_outlier_weight_2026-08-30.md) — 1 Blocker / 3 Majors / 8 Minors applied from two agents, one correctness and one design-fidelity; 1 Major recorded for D3. **The Blocker was a broken intra-doc link, which `cargo clippy` and `cargo test` cannot see and this crate denies**: `cargo doc --no-deps` is back at its 25-link baseline. [key names, the last two](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_key_names_two_2026-08-30.md) — 1 Blocker / 4 Majors applied from two agents, one of them the file's own reader, who guessed all three renamed keys correctly and confirmed all three against the rows; 12 findings recorded and left, none of them this change's. [C2a](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c2a_2026-08-30.md) — Request-changes, 0 Blockers / 4 Majors / 14 Minors from two agents; 9 mutations run, 9 fail a test. [C1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_c1_2026-08-30.md) — Request-changes, 0 Blockers / 6 Majors / 15 Minors from four agents in isolated worktrees, one of them the file's own reader; 8 mutations run, 6 fail a test and 2 proved equivalent. **One reported hole did not reproduce.** [B3](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_b3_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 0 Blockers / 5 Majors / 6 Minors from two agents, one of them the file's own reader; 34 mutations run, 5 survived, all now fail a test. **Four of the five Majors were comments that said something untrue.** [B2](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_b2_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 2 Blockers / 5 Majors / 8 Minors from three agents, one of them a TOML-specification pass and one of them a reading of the produced file as a geneticist; 11 mutations run, 4 survived, all now fail a test. [B1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_b1_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 2 Blockers / 9 Majors / 24 Minors from five agents in isolated worktrees; 23 mutations run, 18 survived, and the six whose behaviour difference was proved all fail a test on the fixed tree. [A3](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_a3_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 1 Blocker / 4 Majors / 4 Minors from two agents, one of them a design-fidelity pass over the whole of Milestone A; 5 mutations run, 1 survived. [A2](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_a2_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 4 Majors / 4 Minors from two category agents; 4 mutations run, 0 survived, so A2's own test is discriminating. [A1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_parameters_file_a1_2026-08-28.md) — Request-changes, 1 Blocker / 13 Majors / 32 Minors as filed by five category agents in isolated worktrees; audit trail in the gitignored `tmp/review_2026-08-28_parameters-file-a1/`.
- **Latest fixes-applied:** [C2a](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_c2a_2026-08-30.md), [C1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_c1_2026-08-30.md), [B3](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_b3_2026-08-28.md), [B2](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_b2_2026-08-28.md), [B1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_b1_2026-08-28.md), [A3](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_a3_2026-08-28.md), [A2](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_a2_2026-08-28.md), [A1](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_ng_parameters_file_a1_2026-08-28.md).
- **The key names revised — seven renames, and a reader pass that found the worked example teaching something false.** Owner's decision of 2026-08-30, on the plan's own trigger: two readers of a produced file had to guess at a key and guessed wrongly. `level` → `share_of_reads_that_slip`, `fraction` → `share_of_reads_from_elsewhere`, `stated_length_spectrum_concentration` → `fallback_length_spectrum_concentration`, `was_declared_by_the_run` → `batching_was_declared`, `slipped_reads` → `expected_slipped_reads`, `rung` → `curve_fitted_on` in repeat tracts only, and `reach`'s three values spelled out to `inside_the_fitted_range` and its siblings. **The rename cost two things worth knowing**: eleven fixture sites broke against the pre-pass's own `Slippage` and `LevelProvenance`, whose fields really are called `level` and `slipped_reads`, so the file's names and the fit's are now deliberately different — the same separation the module already keeps between `Warrant` and `Provenance`; and one comment line went nine characters over the file's stated 80. **⚑ And it made a defect legible that nothing had asserted: three of the four `reach` values in the worked example contradicted the repeat counts printed beside them.** `reach` says whether a stratum's `reference_repeats` fell inside the range its curve was fitted over, and the file prints both on one line; the fixture's curves all shared one range of 5–19, chosen for variant coverage, while the reaches were chosen separately — so a stratum at 6 repeats claimed to be *above* 5–19, one at 11 claimed *below*, one at 30 claimed *inside*. Under the old bare `reach = "inside"` there was nothing to check it against. Each curve now carries a range that makes its own reach true, and `every_reach_in_the_fixture_agrees_with_the_repeats_beside_it` holds it: reverting one range fails five tests. **A further Major and five Minors in the file's comments were applied** — among them the header's one editing instruction, which a row beneath it contradicted, and `curve_fitted_on` offered three values in prose where it can write four, one of them under a name the parser refuses. Tests 78 → 79.
- **C5 done — Milestone C complete. The five states of spec §5 survive the trip, and the answer each collapse changes is not the same kind of thing for all five.** **Two change a number a locus is scored against**: a stratum's length spectrum is the prior every tract of it is seeded from, and a `(stratum × slippage group)` with no row sends the caller to `StutterModel::hipstr_shipped`, which expects **one read in twenty** to come back a repeat short where a slip rate of zero expects **none**. **Three change only what the run says about itself** — and that is the finding rather than a weakness: a defaulted multiplier of 1.0 and a fitted one multiply every read's error probability by the same number, and `likelihood::ssr`'s own test already says the three-term form at a fraction of zero equals the two-term one bit for bit. So the warrant a call folds — `summarise_condition`'s is the only place in calling that reads the calibration's — and the report an output prints are the only things that separate those states, which is what §5's sentence says. **Five mutations against the projection, one a row, each failed its own row's test.** **The review's two Majors were rows asserting through something the caller never reaches**: `Slippage::read_probabilities`, which the fit calls and calling does not, and a warrant `of_run` copies off the calibration with no logic between. Both now go through the caller's own path. Tests 134 → 139.
- **C4 done — a run's parameters, written and read back, are the same run — on a fixture shaped like a fit's, because nothing in this tree produces a real one.** Six stages: parameters → file → text → file → parameters → file, and the two files are byte-identical. **⚑ The real-fit round trip is owed a program, not a test** — the joint walk stops at a `JointFit`, every call to `assemble` is in a test module, and `assemble` needs the minted-error totals the joint route does not fit. **The review's Blocker was that the first draft of the fixture was not shaped like a fit's**: within a period the fitted and derived strata contradicted each other about whether the period had a curve, and `LevelSource::Blend` — a stratum with its own evidence weighed against its period's curve, the fit's ordinary case — appeared nowhere in the file. The first period now has no curves and the other two have both, which is what decides which outcomes each period's strata can have; a whole sample's contamination is refused rather than one library's, which is the only per-unit refusal the contamination fit has; and one library's fitted rate is zero, which is how a run reaches the defaulted scale of 1.0 that spec §5's third row is about. **One field is invisible to the trip and is guarded elsewhere**: the tract ladder's fallback concentration's warrant, which the writer re-derives and `validate` refuses a contradiction in. **⚑ And it measured what spec §9 prices**: 157 bytes an inbreeding row and 185 a substitution-rate row against §9's 146 each, which would put §9's 62 MB at 3,000 samples nearer 79 MB — both floors, since a real cohort's read-group ids and bases-compared counts are wider than this fixture's, and §9's own 146 reproduces nothing in the tree. Tests 130 → 134.
- **C3 done — both formatters recover every double, and the writer that matters did so before this step.** Spec §4 said whether the crate emits enough digits "has not been checked here"; it has now. Twenty-two adversarial values — the two ends of the subnormal range, a negative subnormal, both zeros, the largest finite double, 2⁵³, one unit in the last place above one, and four at a fit's magnitudes — at four structural positions each, plus ten thousand pseudo-random bit patterns, **compared on `to_bits` rather than `==`, which cannot see a lost sign on a zero**. Nothing failed. **The step's standing value is the guard**: mutating the writer's formatter to `{value:.5?}` fails all four new tests and ten older ones. **⚑ Two of the brief's three adversarial categories are covered and the third is not** — no value comes from a real fit, which is C4's under the owner's ruling that C4 runs on constructed inputs, so *the exact concentrations a real fit produces* stays owed with the real-fit test. **The review found no defect in the code and two false claims in the module's own header**, both about its own assertions: that every one compares bits, where the last test compares whole files with `==`; and that the golden files are what check `serde`'s writer, where no test in the module reads a file. Tests 126 → 130.
- **C2 done — a file reads back into the parameters a run scores with, and the round trip is what found four things the fixture claimed that no run could produce.** The projection is `to_run_parameters.rs`, and it needed **three** constructors on types outside this module rather than the two the plan predicted: `RunParameters::of_gathered_values`, `StratumFits::of_gathered_rows` and `SequencingBatches::of_gathered_columns` — the batching's own `declared` takes sets of read groups plus the run's `ReadGroups` table and derives the sample column, which a file has already been through once. **`validate` now runs on the read path**, which it did not before: `to_run_parameters` is its caller. **What the round trip found, and none of it was in the code**: the fixture put one plant's two lanes in two sequencing batches while the per-sample table put that plant in one — a state `SequencingBatches::declared` refuses in memory; it hung an evidence count of 4 reads on a `defaulted` multiplier, where the projection's own rule is that a stated constant has nothing behind it; it marked the tract ladder's fallback `defaulted` at 1.25 in a file that fits a stratum, where the writer states the median of its own fits and marks it `fitted_here`; and it listed the slippage rows in an order the writer does not emit. **Two became refusals and two were corrections.** **⚑ And the review found this caller could write a file its own reader refused**: a run with no repeat tracts declares no slippage group, and `validate` required that table to cover `0..n` — so Milestone E's defaults run and `CLAUDE.md`'s single-sample case both produced one. That table and the substitution rate are checked for naming only the run's read groups now, not for covering them. Tests 103 → 120.
- **C1 done — a file a run wrote reads back, and a broken one names its line.** The reader is `toml::from_str`, as Milestone B established; what C1 builds is **the failing line as data**, because the crate renders "at line N" in its prose and exposes only a byte span — the document text, the key path and the line itself are private, so a caller wanting to *use* the position has to re-derive it, and `from_toml` is the one moment the span and the text are in the same place. **The arithmetic is deliberately the crate's own**, so this module's line and the crate's rendered line are two derivations of one span that must agree; every failure test asserts both, and three review agents proved the equivalence rather than taking the comment's word for it. **Three things were measured rather than assumed.** A refusal raised *after* the document parsed still carries a position, and not for the reason the first draft gave: `set_input` attaches the document **text** and no position — what preserves the span is `set_span`, called in two other files on the way out of every key and value that arrived without one. A *missing* key has no position of its own, so the crate reports it against whatever should have held it — an inline row's key gives the row, a section's key gives that section's **header** (21 bytes, exactly `[ordinary_site_prior]`), and a top-level key gives a zero-width span at byte 0, so **line 1, which in a written file is a comment**. And **a value spanning several lines is reported at the line it opens on** — the only fixture whose span straddles lines, and so the only thing standing between the reader and `span.end`, which passed every other test. **Six of eight mutations fail a test; the two that survive are equivalent** and one of them is a review finding that did not reproduce: an unknown key inside a `blend` table is refused with `deny_unknown_fields` and without it, because the `toml` crate rejects leftover keys in a struct-variant table on its own. Tests 63 → 78.
- **B3 done — Milestone B complete. The file explains itself, and the review that mattered was a reader rather than a compiler.** 80 of the produced file's 161 lines are now explanation: a note above each section in the reader's language, the one rule for editing a value, and a note above each defaulted number saying where its default came from. **Only two of those lines are per-row**, so the cost is fixed rather than one a sample. **Four of the review's five Majors were comments that said something untrue**, and the worst answered the previous reader's own question with the wrong option: the contamination note said "how much of each library's **DNA**" where the quantity is a share of that read group's **reads** and the grain is the lane — and the spec's reason is that index hopping mislabels reads on a flowcell rather than putting anyone's DNA in the tube, so a reader following it would think a four-lane library was uncorrected while three of its lanes still were. Also wrong: "each value carries a warrant", on which the file's one editing rule rested, where eight numbers do and the slippage numbers, both prior concentrations and both length spectra do not; a batching note refuted by the three rows printed under it; and one word, `rung`, given an authoritative definition for one ladder while a second `rung` for another sits twenty lines below. **34 mutations found five survivors and one was a real hole** — the flat concentration's note fired whatever the warrant said. All five now fail a test. **⛦ And B3 cannot deliver spec §8's headline comment**: the slippage defaults' "which alignments, at what depth, on which date" has nowhere to attach, because a slippage number carries a smoothing origin and no warrant, so no state of it is `defaulted`. Same gap as B1's design Major and the item open since Checkpoint A — one ruling closes all three.
- **B2 done — a run's parameters are text, and the first produced file was read by somebody who had not seen the code.** The writer is hand-written because spec §4 chose TOML *for the comments* and no serde serializer emits one; B3 attaches them. **It departs from §4 in one place, which the plan puts in the coder's hands** — §4 suggests the repeat-tract rows be "arrays of arrays", and they are inline tables, because a slippage row carries where its level and its two shares came from and a number off a curve carries the whole curve, so flattened it becomes numbers whose meaning is a column legend. **The review's two Blockers were the same shape**: two of spec §5's absences and both empty-array paths were written by code no test exercised, so a writer that turned an absence into a zero, or dropped an empty table's line, left all 54 tests green — and the second produces a file that will not parse at all. Ten tests became fourteen and all five surviving mutations now fail one. **A specification pass found one shape of document TOML does not define**: a count above 2⁶³−1 gave three answers from three readers — this crate's derived reader accepted it, `toml::Value` refused it, Python's `tomllib` took it — so every integer now goes through one helper that saturates at TOML's largest, which announces itself. **And the claim that a bare `1` would not read back as an `f64` was measured false** — this crate's reader takes it — so the reason the floats carry a decimal point is now the true one: `toml::Value` and every other language's parser type `1` as an integer, and a file whose type depends on who opened it is not the artefact goal 3 describes. **Four more numbers in the step's own prose were wrong** and the reader caught all four against sixteen it re-derived and found right.
- **B1 done — and the projection found a state the file could not express.** The seed the ordinary-site prior starts from records which of **four** things it rests on (`SeedRegime`) and the file's `SeedRung` was built with **three**: the missing one is a cohort with **no variation at all**, whose pair of concentrations is a legal pair that says nothing about how it was arrived at. A run there had no rung to be written under. Added, and the guard is now structural — **all eight conversions from a pre-pass word to its file spelling are exhaustive matches**, so a state added upstream stops compiling. **Exhaustiveness cannot catch a state *crossed* with another** (`Flat => Sloping` compiles and spells correctly), which is what `every_pre_pass_word_maps_to_its_own_word_in_the_file` names pair by pair. **The projection reads two things `RunParameters` does not hold** — the calibration's read count and every sample's inbreeding warrant and count — so it takes the pre-pass's estimates beside the assembled parameters, which is what A2 predicted. **The review's two Blockers were both about the tests, and both were measured:** one `#[should_panic]` test could not fail (its expected substring matched both of the two guards, and its fixture tripped both), and the calibration row's evidence count was joined to an argument nothing checked — a rate map from another fit was accepted and wrote `Reads(7)` beside a multiplier built from 812,344 reads. Sixteen tests became thirty-one, and the six mutations the review proved survived now each fail a test. **Four numbers in the step's own prose were wrong** and the naming pass caught all four, against ten it re-derived and found right — among them "counts five orders of magnitude apart" for a factor of 19.
- **A3 done — Milestone A complete. The five states of spec §5 are expressible and distinct, and a design-fidelity pass says the types cover §3 in full.** The two contamination states are the work: an uncontaminated run writes **no `[contamination]` section**, and a read group that identified nothing has **no `measurement`** where in memory it has a zero fraction beside two zero counts that `ContaminationView::was_measured` has to be asked about. That also drops a wart `run_parameters.rs` documents and cannot fix — an unmeasured read group there still carries a `ContaminationSource`, and neither variant is true of it. **⛦ The step's own test could not fail on the hazard its own message named.** Part 5 asserted a property of `Vec::pop` over data it had just edited and never read the emitted document; a writer densifying the (stratum × slippage group) axis with zero rows — §5's fifth row's exact prohibition — added three rows to the file and left it green. Parts 4 and 5 now read the document, on the gap the fixture already has, and the same mutation fails them. **Only the golden file had been catching it**, and it killed four of the review's five mutants. **Two shapes remain writable that no run should mean** — a contamination table whose every row is unmeasured, and a measurement whose counts are both zero — and neither can be made unspellable by a type; both are **step C2's to refuse**, and a test pins that they are accepted today so C2 inverts a failing test rather than adding a forgotten one.
- **A2 done (one shape for a value and its warrant) — and the review found three claims wrong, one of them A1's.** Five numbers now carry `WarrantedValue`, where the first draft had four: **`repeat_tract_outlier_weight` was left out**, and spec §8 names it among the three parameters with an honest default that must all be marked. It has two reachable states — the project's 0.01, and whatever a person types into the file, which spec §3.8 says is the point of writing it down — and without a warrant a run reports the second as the first. **The evidence count now names its unit in the file** (`observations = { covered_positions = 180600412 }`) rather than in a doc comment, because the doc comment gave the wrong unit for two of the three quantities: an inbreeding coefficient is fitted over covered reference positions and not over windows, which are 100,000 bases each, and a repeat-tract substitution rate over bases compared and not over reads. **And A1 shipped a claim that spec §3.3 contradicts** — that the base-quality calibration has no observation count. `ReadGroupCalibration` has none, but the fit's `Estimate<ErrorRate>` does and `RunParameters::assemble` drops it; A2 built to A1's wrong requirement and its test then pinned it. Fixed forward. **Two of the five numbers therefore need data `RunParameters` does not hold, so step B1 projects from the pre-pass's estimates rather than from `RunParameters` alone.** Eleven tests became thirteen.
- **A1 done (the shape and the provisional TOML tree) — and the review found the tests could not fail on most of what they claimed.** Of fourteen mutations the four original tests killed **one**. A `serde` round trip writes and reads through the same derive, so it moves both sides of any rename together: dropping `#[serde(rename_all)]` from five of the seven enums, and renaming any of about sixty struct fields, each changed the file on disk and left every test green. **A golden file is what pins a key surface**, and there is now one, plus a table over all 21 unit variants. **The module's own fixture wrote an illegal state**: a `curve: None` beside a `reach: Some(BelowFitted)`, a claim about a curve's fitted range in a row recording no curve — three of the four combinations were meaningless and all four were writable. The curve and the reach now ride on the smoothing variant, which is the device the blend's weight already used. **And a typo in a hand-edited file read back as a fitted fact**: with no `deny_unknown_fields`, misspelling the `curve` table by one letter parses and yields `curve: None`, which the module's own doc defines as *this stratum's period had no curve*. That attribute is a *type* attribute with no call-site knob, so it could not have waited for step C's reader. Four tests became eleven; the module went 960 → 1,508 lines. **Two of the review's own survivors were re-run against the fixed tree and both now fail.**
- **Open:**

It is `DEFAULT_ERROR_PROBABILITY_MULTIPLIER` now. **The inventory in `parameters_file/defaults.rs` is seven rows and not §8's four**, because `to_toml.rs`'s `origins` module — written at B3 *"so that step E1 has one list to reconcile against"* — already carried the repeat-tract substitution rate, the slippage numbers and the inbreeding coefficient; the prior's seed is outside the table because what marks its fallback is `SeedRegime::FallbackDiversity` and not a warrant. **⚑ The Blocker was the rung refusing a file this caller writes.** On the outlier weight and the fallback concentration `defaulted` means *the run took this compiled-in number*, so the warrant fixes the value; on a multiplier it is copied from the *error rate* the multiplier was built from, and the pre-pass's rate ladder has a `Defaulted` bottom rung of its own (`DEFAULT_ERROR_RATE`, 0.001) — so a legitimate run writes `defaulted` beside `0.001 / that library's mean minted error`. **Measured: 0.125 and 0.25 on two libraries averaging 0.008 and 0.004.** The way in is a read group under `MIN_SITES_TO_FIT` = 10,000 sites with no sibling to borrow from — one gene, a panel, a shallow single sample, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s hardest corner — and the symptom is the run's whole parameters file becoming unreadable. `a_run_whose_rates_were_defaulted_writes_a_file_its_own_reader_accepts` stops the rung coming back. **Four refusal messages were re-worded in the same commit**, all found by reading the produced file as a user: the three `defaulted` refusals now close with one clause word for word (the concentration's said *"a number **somebody chose** is `supplied`"* where the others give an instruction), and four printed `is 0,` and `is 1` where the file writes `0.0` and `1.0`. Tests 170 → 177.

Declared into group 0 the answer is `NoSuchStratum`, which is ordinary; both reviewers re-derived it. **⛑ The writer could not write such a run at all**, so two of its assertions were relaxed, each still a tightening for every warrant but `Defaulted`: the rates map covers every read group or is empty (a *short* map is the one to refuse), and a missing rate is legal exactly where that read group's calibration is `Defaulted`, the one warrant that writes no count. The correctness review proved no count can move: on a mixed-warrant fixture, a rate set that drops one entry and carries a stranger writes a **byte-identical** file. **A `supplied` value with a zero count now writes no `observations`** — an operator's declared coefficient was claiming a measurement over no genome, against the file's own editing rule; a *fitted* zero still writes, because a fit that produced a number from no reads is alarming and the count is what says so. **⛑ The author's first draft asserted every coefficient against the constant rather than a literal zero**, so a build shipping it at 0.5 passed all 184 tests; three assertions compare against a literal now and it fails all three. The mismatch predates E2, which only made the second source work. **The shape of the fix is a signature change**: `of_run` wants what the *file* holds — a value, a warrant and a count per read group — rather than the fit's `Estimate<ErrorRate>`, which it reads for the count alone. Checking that turned up a fact the file now carries — at a slip share of 0.10 a fitted row would hold 0.005 of part-repeat mass and the shipped model holds **0.02**, four times as much, so the shipped model is not a point the fit could produce. Both belong with F1, which owns the writer and is where a run knows which of §7's three sources it came from. **Contamination's absence is fixed at E3** — the note explaining what a missing `[contamination]` section means used to live *inside* the section, so the one reader who needed it never saw it and the word appeared nowhere in a defaults run's file.
  - **The inbreeding coefficient of a defaults run — RULED 2026-08-31 (owner): F defaults to 0, and a user may set one value for the whole run or a different value for any sample.** So there is a fourth compiled-in default, `0.0`, the value at which the genotype prior's `1 − F` factor does nothing — the same kind of default as a multiplier of one rather than a guess at a quantity. A coefficient nobody stated is `defaulted`; one the user stated is `supplied`, and a per-sample override is a `supplied` row beside `defaulted` ones. **Two texts say the opposite today and are true of the *fit* rather than of the run**, so E2 makes each say which it is about rather than deleting either: `parameter_estimation::generic::fallback`'s header (*"The inbreeding coefficient has one rung and it is not a default … a cohort's diversity divides by `1 − F`, so a wrong constant would be amplified rather than absorbed"*) — still true, the fit will not invent one from data it does not have — and `to_toml`'s `origins::INBREEDING_COEFFICIENT`, which prints *"a run should not be able to write this line"* beside a defaulted coefficient in the file and now can. *(Was: a blocking question with three options; the recommendation was to take it as an argument, and the ruling is the second option plus the per-sample override.)*

The tree instead charges it the pre-pass's stated `DEFAULT_ERROR_RATE` of 0.001, by building the multiplier as `0.001 / that library's own mean minted error`. **The owner's reason: a library's real error rate is never its reported sequencing quality**, because the quality scores describe base calling while the reads also carry mismapping, chimeras and damage — so a library nothing could be fitted for should be charged a stated rate rather than taken at its word. 0.001 stands as a placeholder and **will be replaced by a value fitted from GIAB**, alongside §8's slippage measurement. **⚑ And the direction was reported backwards in chat before the ruling**, off a fixture claiming 0.008 — Q21, unlike any library in this project — which made the multiplier 0.125 and the reads *more* confident.   - **The repeat-tract outlier weight — RULED and landed 2026-08-30 (owner): wire it, in its own commit, before F1.** Spec §3.8 puts this number in the file precisely so that a person can change it, and until now `RunParameters` had no field for it, `report()` hard-coded the compiled-in constant and the scoring path read the same constant — so an edited file round-tripped and changed nothing. It now rides on the run as a `RepeatTractOutlierWeight` (a value and its warrant, beside `DEFAULT_OUTLIER_WEIGHT` in `likelihood/ssr.rs`), reaches scoring through `FrozenParameters` → `TractScoringFits::gather_for_locus`, and the file's projection writes the run's value and warrant rather than the constant. **Two of the four warrants are refusals for this key** — nothing fits it — and so is a `defaulted` value that is not the constant, which is what a person who changes the number and leaves the warrant alone produces. **The two review findings worth carrying:** deleting the forwarding call from *one* of `view()`'s two arms left the whole suite green until a contaminated run was added to the test; and `validate` accepted a weight of exactly zero, which the scoring row panics on — zero being the most likely edit to a key called an outlier weight. **⚑ Owed at D3**: spec §2.1 demotes a mismatched file's numbers to `Supplied` wholesale, and for this one number that is a *promotion* — `Provenance::strength` ranks `Defaulted` 0 and `Supplied` 1 — so the demotion must be `weaker_of(file_warrant, Supplied)`, a no-op for every other number.
